- Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
- Action: re-checked the HA dev box (HA OS, HA 2026.11.2) about 10 minutes after deploying #39 (a80698f).
- Outcome (VERIFIED):

  - 431 tigo entities present.

  - Daily energy 109.18 → 154.93 Wh; total energy 2171534.06 → 2171580.47 Wh; consistent with ~0.3 kW, no jumps.
- New #39 entities per panel observed:
  - `binary_sensor` `high_output_mode`: on for 26/26 panels.

- CORRECTION: this supersedes the U2 caveat wording "dusk (~0.35 W)". The earlier reading was a unit misread; actual power is ~0.30–0.36 **kW** at low afternoon production (not dusk, not ~0.35 W). Earlier U0/U2 entries are not rewritten.

## U5 — W3 (impact-38-39, gemini-4-carbon) handoff: #38 and #39

- Timestamp: 2026-11-22T15:08Z
- Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
- Worker: W3 impact-38-39. Evidence status: agent-reported; verified via scratch tests in WSL (not independently re-verified by coordinator).
- Scratch evidence (WSL, machine-local, not in repo): `~/pytap-test/scratch-impact-38-39/wt/tests/test_scratch_impact_38_39.py` — 4 passed, each demonstrating a defect.

### #38 findings
- Adds 6 admin services. Captures to `/config/pytap_captures` (defaults 24 h / 1 GiB, max 4 GiB, never auto-pruned).
- HA backup bloat: CONFIRMED.
- `..` path traversal: REFUTED.
- Parent-dir symlink bypass: CONFIRMED. `capture.py` `_check_root` checks the leaf only; write/export/rmtree can land outside `/config`.
- Separable: parser clock param + `replay.py` + `scripts/replay_capture.py` (~450 LOC).
- Recommendation: decline the capture services; optionally keep the replay dev tooling (effort S).

### #39 findings
- 4 entities per panel, all diagnostic, enabled by default.
- Dedup OK, except a sustained fault rewrites `last_fault_at` on every report (write churn).
- `reading_faults()` discards the whole report when V ∉ [0,100], Vout > Vin + 2 V, or temp ∉ [−40,125]. Loss of valid readings/energy CONFIRMED by scratch test (e.g. 80.8/78.5 V pass-through; 204.7 °C open thermistor). A discard lasting >120 s loses all energy and the panel goes stale.
- `high_output_mode` on 26/26 at low output (meaning doubtful); `low_voltage_flag` is redundant with `status_code`.
- #39 has no code dependency on #38.
- #39 violates roadmap §6 (`planning/future_considerations.md`, binary sensor platform "will not implement").
- Recommendation: decline entities/binary_sensor; rework/defer the fault filter to conservative bounds without dropping energy (effort S).

- `SerialPortSelector` exists from HA 2026.9 — prior agent claim PARTIALLY REFUTED.

- serialx is a pure-python wheel.

### #37 — recommendation: ACCEPT WITH REQUIRED CHANGES (effort M), rebase onto #35 (no serial dependency)
- Migrations v4 → v7 preserve modules and unique IDs; existing `auto_discovery=True` survives; downgrade requires a full backup restore.
- Three scratch regressions FAIL (required fixes):
  1. Discovery can claim a barcode owned by another unloaded entry.
  2. Options "Done" resurrects a module deleted elsewhere.
  3. Need shared-bus/admission warnings and a preference for opt-in discovery.
- String label edits create new aggregate IDs (history not transferred).
- Dual test harness (2026.10.4 pytest + 2026.11 smoke) judged not sustainable.

- **BLOCKING — #37:** a third bug was omitted from the report (corresponds to GPT finding 2 in U8: constructor claim failure only logs).

### Coordinator verification (VERIFIED)
- DoubleDoc fork schema (via `gh api`): v6 = inverter, v7 = connection_type, v8 = discovery, with idempotent `5 <= v < 8` `setdefault` migrations.

  - Timestamp: 2026-11-22T17:36Z (logged 2026-11-22T17:36Z wall-clock)
  - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork

  - Reviews: both independent implementation reviews are complete (reviewer-reported; not yet reconciled into code changes). No code changed since the reviews.

  ### Claude (Opus 5.5) — no blocking issue; one design ambiguity
  - Rare crash-recovery ambiguity: HA aggregate baselines may be *newer* than the coordinator's delayed Store. After a crash, an apparent decrease may be a stale restore rather than a genuine reset, and crediting the full post-"reset" value could overcount.
  - Also noted: no remove/re-add regression test.

  ### Gemini (4 Carbon) — findings
  - **BLOCKING restore path:** `_async_restore_energy` does not prune baselines to current members. If no remaining member has node data, `async_added_to_hass` skips accumulation, so removed members' baselines persist and the daily cycle does not roll on restore.
  - Also noted: no remove/re-add regression test (agrees with Claude).
  - Timezone race in the two `test_sensor.py` fixtures updated in U23 (HA test timezone vs the unattached sensor's UTC fallback) — fixtures need hardening.
  - Asked to preserve the baseline/restart/`last_reset` headline in the 1.4.0 changelog.

  ### Decision pending from user (ATTEMPTED; awaiting answer)
  - Reset-vs-stale-restore ambiguity — options put to the user:
    1. Keep exact credit (current PRD v3 behaviour) and document the risk.
    2. Trade credit for possible energy loss (conservative; do not credit on ambiguous decrease).
    3. Expand persistence metadata to disambiguate (larger change; likely needs PRD update/re-approval).
  - Rationale for blocking: the choice changes the approved PRD v3 reset semantics ("credit exactly the post-reset value"), so a material scope/behaviour decision belongs to the user.

  ### Next steps (PLANNED, after the decision)
  1. Update PRD if behaviour changes (re-approval of affected requirements).
  2. Add failing tests first (restore-path pruning; remove/re-add member; fix timezone-race fixtures), then implement.
  3. Update the changelog headline; re-run full tests/Ruff; re-review materially changed areas only.
  4. Minimum-HA 2025.3 verification; HA dev-box restore pre-step and 24 h soak; 1.4.0 release/tag.

  1. A current configured member skipped *only* because its `daily_reset_date` is stale must remain tracked with a **zero baseline**, so its first current-day value is credited. The no-credit initial rebase must be reserved for **newly added** membership only.
  2. `PyTapSensor` has no `_today()` method, so the timezone rule should be phrased around `_timezone()` and `last_reset`, with `_today()` only where it exists (the aggregate sensor).

  ### Status / next steps
  - Awaiting Gemini's independent v5 review before resolving and finalising the wording (PENDING).
  - Then request explicit user approval of the final PRD version; implementation remains unauthorised until then (PLANNED).
  - No code or test changes.

  ### Blocking findings
  1. **New / re-added daily member incorrectly credited.** The stale-date current-member zero baseline (introduced in U34) also marks a genuinely new/re-added daily member as initialized, so its first eligible current-day reading is credited, contradicting the PRD v6 no-credit rebase rule for new/re-added membership. (Distinction required: stale-date *current* member ⇒ zero baseline, credit first matching reading; *new/re-added* member ⇒ no-credit initial rebase.)
  2. **Zero baseline lost when `_energy_total` is still None.** An intervening snapshot that omits the stale member clears its zero baseline; when initial seeding later happens, that member's first eligible energy is dropped.
  - Both fall within PRD v6's currently conflicting stated invariants; they need regression tests (TDD red) and fixes (green).

  ### Other items still open
  - Claude's suggestions (U39): tighten the local-midnight `last_reset` test (exact value; restore just after non-UTC midnight) and document the overcount magnitude.
  - Reconciliation status: Claude (U39) reported no blockers; GPT (this entry) found two blockers — the pair-review reconciliation is needed; the blocking items stand until fixed and re-reviewed.

  ### Accepted-risk quantification (from GPT)
  - The accepted false-reset (stale-restore) overcount could be up to the restored member counter value: potentially the lifetime total for total-energy counters; the current-day total for daily-energy counters.

  ### Decision pending from user (ATTEMPTED; awaiting answer)
  - Whether to retain exact credit while explicitly documenting this magnitude (consistent with the U26/U28 decision), or pause to reconsider the risk. No replacement behaviour approved.

  ### Next steps (PLANNED)
  1. Obtain the user's answer on the risk question.

  1. **Distinguish the deployment restart from the soak's single graceful restart.** Record membership and totals before and after deployment, and require no decrease.

  ## U83 — PRD v8 review 2 of 2 (Gemini 4 Carbon): do not approve pending fixes; storage-accounting discrepancy with review 1

  - Timestamp: 2026-11-23T17:11Z (coordinator's `current_datetime` 2026-11-23T17:11:08Z; the message carried the ID U83)
  - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
  - Task: PYTAP-140-00 (PRD v8 review) — on the board in **Review**. PRD v8 is a candidate, **not approved**.

  ### Review outcome (VERIFIED as received; every finding below is **reviewer-reported** and was not independently verified (the coordinator asked that they be cited as reviewer findings)
  - Reviewer: **Gemini 4 Carbon**, read-only, on `docs/prd-1.4.0-robust-tcp-core-v8.md`. Disposition: **do not approve pending fixes**.
  - Both required reviews are now in (Claude Opus 5.5 in U82, Gemini 4 Carbon here).

  ### Storage-accounting findings (disagrees in part with U79 and U82's framing; needs reconciling before v8 is finalized)
  - The v8 storage description is **inaccurate**. The service default is **1024 MiB**, but the budget is **configurable from 16 to 4096 MiB**, not a fixed 1 GiB.
  - The reviewer says **exports count in the retained-root accounting**, but the **export calculation excludes only the source session directory**. A source session plus its archive can therefore **exceed the nominal budget, up to about 2x**.
  - **No automatic pruning** and **backup inclusion** are confirmed as before (U79).
  - **Open discrepancy:** U79 and the earlier phrasing say exports are "additional and excluded from the budget" (the HA Developer fact-check relayed in U79). The Gemini finding says exports count toward retained-root accounting but the export-time estimate omits only the source session. The two statements are not yet reconciled; **the coordinator is awaiting a clarification from Claude** before finalizing the storage wording. Until then, treat the "exports additional" description as **unconfirmed**.

  ### Compatibility findings
  - `requirements-test` 0.13.375 **overrides HA 2026.10.4's `serialx` 1.11.0 with 1.15.1** (matches U82's blocking finding). **CI checks 2026.11.0**.

  ## U84 — Storage-accounting discrepancy reconciled (Claude confirms Gemini's reading); fresh user confirmation required

  - Timestamp: 2026-11-23T17:12Z (coordinator's `current_datetime` 2026-11-23T17:12:19Z; the message carried the ID U84)
  - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
  - Task: PYTAP-140-00 (PRD v8 review) — on the board in **Review**. PRD v8 is a candidate, **not approved**.

  ### Reconciliation (reviewer-reported via a same-task Claude clarification; sources not independently re-verified by the logger)
  - The open discrepancy in U83 is resolved: **Claude's clarification confirms Gemini's storage reading.** Gemini's storage finding is confirmed. Claude's initial blocking finding (B1 in U82) was about HA minimum-version **test reliability** (the `serialx` harness override), not storage.
  - Confirmed storage facts:
    - `_tree_size` counts **all regular files, including ZIP archives**.
    - `max_storage_mib` **defaults to 1024 MiB** but is **configurable from 16 to 4096 MiB per capture start**.
    - Capture start **subtracts the files already in the capture root**.
    - The export budget **excludes only the source session directory**, so a source session plus its archive can reach **about 2x** the nominal budget.
    - The archive **then counts toward future capture and export budgets**.
    - **Delete removes a capture and its archives.**
    - There is **no automatic pruning** (as in U79).
  - **Correction to U79's storage wording:** U79 recorded exports as "additional and excluded from the budget" (the HA Developer fact-check). That is **not accurate**. Exports are not fully extra or unbudgeted: they count in later root accounting, and only the export-time estimate leaves out the source session directory. U79 and U83's "unconfirmed" label are superseded by this entry.
  - **Claude's view on the user-confirmed statement:** the v8 statement that exports are **fully extra / unbudgeted** was **based on incorrect facts**, so the user's confirmation of it (U79: "specifically selected preserving storage policy") **needs fresh user confirmation** on the corrected facts.

  ### Sources cited by the reviewers (agent-reported; the mapping of each ref to a statement was not given to the logger, and none was re-checked)
  - `capture.py:118-120,232-241,862-865,925-950,991-995`
  - `capture_services.py:146`
  - `coordinator.py:675,772-785`
  - `tests/test_capture_integrity.py:230-240`
  - `tests/test_review2_capture.py:206,222-245,275-288`
  - `tests/test_packet_capture.py:88-100`

  ### Decision needed from the user (PENDING; nothing is approved)
  - Whether to **preserve this exact behaviour** (document it accurately in v8: configurable 16–4096 MiB budget, default 1024 MiB, source plus archive up to about 2x at export, no auto-pruning, included in backups), or **change to a strict combined cap**. The coordinator will ask the user.

  ### State
  - **No PRD or board changes yet** in response. No code changes, no HA state changes. The soak is **not started**.

  ### Next steps (PLANNED)
  - Obtain the user's choice on the storage policy, then reconcile both reviews and revise v8. Revisions include the accurate storage wording or a strict-cap requirement, the `serialx` and no-override 2026.10.4 test method, the `port_name` / `port_udev_id` behaviour, the feasibility-gate task, the soak precision and rollback plan, capture containment at `hass.config.config_dir`, manual-claim handling, the version references, and the release-artifact ordering (U82, U83).
  - Record the disposition of each material finding, then request explicit user approval of the v8 version before any implementation.

    ## U85 — Both PRD v8 reviews reconciled; user decisions on storage, ESPHome URLs and manual claims

    - Timestamp: 2026-11-23T17:17Z (coordinator's `current_datetime` 2026-11-23T17:17:37Z; the message carried the ID U85)
    - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
    - Task: PYTAP-140-00 (PRD v8 review) — on the board in **Review**. PRD v8 is a candidate, **not approved**. This entry consolidates U82–U84 and adds the user's decisions.

    ### Review dispositions (VERIFIED as received; findings are reviewer-reported)
    - **Claude Opus 5.5:** approve with changes (U82).
    - **Gemini 4 Carbon:** do not approve pending fixes (U83).
    - **Consolidated material issues:**
      - False-pass HA 2026.10.4 tests (the `serialx` harness override).
      - Storage misdescription in v8.
      - HA-version-specific ESPHome URL support.
      - Deployment restart vs the soak's graceful restart, and the failure/rollback path.
      - Exact schema evidence (`.storage/core.config_entries`; stop if above v7).
      - Capture symlink trusted anchor, and coverage of list, export, delete and root removal.
      - Manual-claim policy.

      - `const.py` and README version metadata.

    ### User decisions (APPROVED as scope decisions; they are not a PRD approval)
    1. **Storage: preserve the exact #39 behaviour** (chosen after the corrected facts were presented):
       - default **1024 MiB**, configurable **16–4096 MiB per capture start**;
       - **existing archives are counted** in later budgets;
       - **export self-exclusion only** (the source session directory);
       - **no pruning**;
       - **deletion removes the capture and its archives**;
       - **captures are included in HA backups**.
       - This replaces the invalid "exports fully extra/unbudgeted" confirmation from U79 (see U84). The strict combined cap was not chosen.
    2. **ESPHome stored URLs: use version-native formats.**
       - **HA 2026.10.4:** `port_name`.
       - **HA 2026.11+:** **preserve `port_udev_id`**.
       - A **stored URL that the running HA version does not support** gets an **explicit, actionable error**.
       - Rationale: resolves the U83 finding that `port_udev_id` filters are not in 2026.10.4's public API, without raising the floor.
    3. **Manual-claim conflict policy:**
       - **disable only the conflicting barcode**;
       - **keep healthy modules running**;
       - surface **one warning/diagnostic**;
       - **prevent duplicate processing**.
       - Rationale: addresses the failed-manual-claim / double-tracking defect and the soak's no-ERROR-log requirement (U83).

    ## U103 — ESPHome import gap verified on both HA versions; filtered-URL hazard confirmed; v9 reconciliation scope

    - Timestamp: 2026-11-23T21:01Z (coordinator's `current_datetime` 2026-11-23T21:01:04Z; UTC)
    - Session: 72475d47-6f0e-4206-9387-b6e4948802a4
    - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
    - Update ID: U103 (follows U102)
    - Task: PYTAP-140-01 — remains **Blocked**. PRD v9 is a draft, **not approved**. Scope of this update: feasibility evidence and PRD v9 reconciliation; verification and documentation only, with no source, live HA or deployment changes.

    ### The ESPHome dependency gap (VERIFIED by the coordinator)
    - Gemini's U102 finding (C3/C4 need ESPHome component dependencies absent from the core-only environment) is **confirmed**.
    - Importing `homeassistant.components.esphome.serial_proxy` **failed in both** isolated core venvs: HA **2026.10.4** and HA **2026.11.2**.
    - **Attempted remedy and outcome:** in each venv the coordinator installed that HA version's declared ESPHome Python requirements (`aioesphomeapi`, `esphome-dashboard-api`, `bleak-esphome`) plus `hassil`, under that version's official constraints.
      - `pip check` **stayed clean** in both.
      - The import **still failed**, now at a missing **`gazetteer_matcher`** module.
    - **Conclusion at this time:**
      - The earlier **7-test HA 2026.11.2 core smoke pass (U99/U100) remains valid** as what it was: supplemental evidence from a core-only environment.
      - **Targeted ESPHome handler coverage and the dependency closure are NOT yet established** on either version. The gate cannot yet be declared established for the ESPHome path (C3/C4).
    - Not given to the logger: how the remaining dependency closure (`gazetteer_matcher` and anything beyond it) will be resolved. This is not recorded as a plan.

    ### ESPHome transport API differs between the two versions (VERIFIED by the coordinator)
    - HA **2026.10.4**: the public `register_serialx_transport` **omits `connect_kwargs`**.
    - HA **2026.11.2**: it **passes `connect_kwargs`**.
    - **Consequence (the coordinator's conclusion):** a stored or filtered ESPHome serial URL must **never be silently accepted with its filters dropped** on the older version. This is consistent with the U85 policy (`port_name` on 2026.10.4; identity filters and `port_udev_id` are 2026.11+ only; an unsupported stored URL gets an actionable error).

    ### v9 reconciliation (REVIEWER-REPORTED findings from U102; corrections PLANNED)
    - GPT-6.1 Sol and Gemini 4 Carbon findings on draft v9 (U102) are being reconciled by a **Claude Opus 5.5 revision**. The planned corrections, as relayed (**not yet written or reviewed**):
      - **Authority:** suspend implementation under **both** v8 and v9 until v9 is approved (addresses GPT's ambiguous-authority finding).
      - **C7:** correct the lifecycle semantics (the draft wrongly mapped setup failures generically to `ConfigEntryNotReady`). The exact replacement wording was not given to the logger.
      - **C8:** replace the unbounded HA API audit with a **bounded 2026.11.2 check that the exact #39 rollback state is readable**.
      - **Manifest `serialx` check:** add a check of the manifest's `serialx` requirement (against the HA 2026.10.4 floor).
      - **URL matrix:** make it **finite** (addresses GPT's unbounded-matrix finding).
      - **ESPHome:** require the **dependency closure and import to be verified before porting** (addresses Gemini's finding).
    - **Both reviewer families (GPT-6.1 Sol and Gemini 4 Carbon) will rerun** on the corrected sections, per the standing process.

    - **No implementation authorized.** PYTAP-140-01 stays Blocked. No code, live HA or deployment changes; the soak has not started.

    ## U104 — Second-round v9 reviews: new URL/lifecycle/rollback-CI findings; correction to U103's `connect_kwargs` inference

    - Timestamp: 2026-11-23T21:25Z (coordinator's `current_datetime` 2026-11-23T21:25:12Z; UTC)
    - Session: 72475d47-6f0e-4206-9387-b6e4948802a4
    - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
    - Update ID: U104 (follows U103)
    - Task: PYTAP-140-01 — remains **Blocked**. PRD v9 is a draft, **not approved**; v8 remains the approved document. Scope of this update: review findings and a correction; documentation only, with no source, live HA or deployment changes.

    ### CORRECTION to U103 (coordinator; supersedes the inference, not the verified facts)
    - U103 recorded as verified that HA 2026.10.4's public `register_serialx_transport` omits `connect_kwargs` while 2026.11.2 passes them. That observation stands.
    - U103 also recorded the **inference** that a filtered ESPHome URL could therefore have its filters silently dropped on 2026.10.4. The coordinator now says this inference is **not established**:
      - `connect_kwargs` governs the **Python keyword arguments** passed to the transport, **not URL query matchers**.
      - HA 2026.10.4 **requires `port_name`**, but **serialx 1.11 itself parses query matchers**. So omitting `connect_kwargs` does not show that URL filters are dropped.
    - **Instruction to the record:** do **not** claim that combined forms drop filters. The plan is to **test enforcement** (what the accepted and rejected URL forms actually do on each version) rather than assert a drop.
    - U103's "Consequence" line is therefore superseded. The earlier text is left in place (append-only).

    ### Second-round review findings on v9 (REVIEWER-REPORTED; not independently verified by the logger)
    - Reviewers: **GPT-6.1 Sol** and **Gemini 4 Carbon**.
    - **GPT-6.1 Sol: URL rows are incomplete.**
      - The literal U1–U4 rows **omit PR #39's nine `_PROXY_FILTERS`** and the **fallback multi-filter URLs** of the HA 2026.11.2 selector.
      - Suggested shape: test **bounded equivalence classes**, **all nine matchers**, and **individual mismatches**, **without enumerating every combination**.
    - **Gemini 4 Carbon: C4 over-requires serialx traversal.**
      - C4 cannot require **every row** to traverse serialx, because **prevalidation rejects before it**.
      - **Accepted rows** use the path `open_source` → serialx → public handler.
      - **Rejected rows** assert **early failure and no serialx call**.
    - **Lifecycle findings** (the relay did not say which reviewer made each):
      - **Preserve** PR #39's config-flow behaviour: `cannot_connect` redaction and the second-submit behaviour.
      - **Preserve** PR #39's unloaded-entry background retry.
      - **Distinguish** a direct `async_setup_entry` raising `ConfigEntryError` from the native `ConfigEntries` **SETUP_ERROR** state (the earlier C7 wording conflated them; see U102).
    - **Gemini 4 Carbon: rollback CI hardening.**
      - The CI must have the **exact #39 SHA available despite a shallow checkout**.
      - **Subprocess isolation** for the baseline-module imports.
      - **Real accumulator and restore writes** (not mocks) in the rollback test.

    ### Reconciliation (ATTEMPTED; underway, no outcome recorded)
    - A **Claude Opus 5.5 revision** of v9 is underway. It will apply the corrections above, plus:
      - an assertion of the **manifest requirement** (the `serialx` manifest check planned in U103);
      - a **deterministic HA component-manifest dependency closure** for the ESPHome path (the planned way to settle the closure/import question left open in U103);
      - resolution of the **earlier blockers** (U102/U103).
    - **Both independent reviewer families will rerun** afterwards, per the standing process. The revision has not yet been written or reviewed.

    ### State
    - v8 is approved; v9 is a draft, not approved. **No implementation authorized.** PYTAP-140-01 stays Blocked.
    - No code, live HA or deployment changes. The soak has not started.
    - The dedicated temp root, venvs and WSL temp copies remain (to be cleaned up at task end).

    ### Next steps (PLANNED)
    - Claude Opus 5.5 completes the v9 revision; GPT-6.1 Sol and Gemini 4 Carbon rerun; reconcile findings; present the exact v9 to the user for explicit approval.

    ## U113 — Tenth-round review of v9 r10: one blocker from each reviewer (test clock handling); r11 revision to follow

    - Timestamp: 2026-11-24T11:46Z (logger-observed UTC; the message carried no `current_datetime`)
    - Session: 72475d47-6f0e-4206-9387-b6e4948802a4
    - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
    - Update ID: U113 (follows U112)
    - Task: PYTAP-140-00 (PRD review/approval) is **In Progress**; PYTAP-140-01 remains **Blocked**. PRD v9 is a draft, **not approved**; v8 remains the approved document. Scope of this update: review findings and a planned revision; documentation only, with no code, tests or live HA work.

    ### Tenth-round reviews of v9 r10 (REVIEWER-REPORTED; independent; not independently verified by the logger)
    - **GPT-6.1 Sol — one blocker:**
      - the r10 **`utcnow`-only patch misses `dt_util.now`**, which **#39's energy and cycle-date paths use**.
      - **Proposed:** patch **`now` and `utcnow` together**.
      - **Non-blocking:**
        - compare the **raw `State` string** and the **raw extra data independently**;
        - compare the **current state with the current `native_value`**;
        - assert **rounding specifically after a coordinator update**.
    - **Gemini 4 Carbon — one blocker:**
      - the clock must be **controllable / steppable**. A **single fixed timestamp** prevents **real accumulation**, because the **energy gap requires 0 < dt <= 300 s**, and it **cannot produce a stale daily member**.
      - **Recommends:** drive **both `now` and `utcnow` from a deterministic, controllable UTC clock**; **advance it between readings** and **across local midnight in the candidate phase**; and **align the save/restore day**.
      - **Non-blocking:**
        - **timezone type normalization**;
        - the **removed-member fixture should retain at least one configured member with saved node data**.
    - **Both reviewers approve the other r9 fixes, the v8 scope and the user choices.** Both blockers concern the test clock, not product scope.

    - The **v8 scope and the user's choices stand**, including the explicit `-portN` rejection on HA 2026.10.4 (U106). Nothing in this round changes scope.

      - **restore HA's cached TZ with the public `set_default_time_zone` in `finally`**;
      - **constrain "removed-member exceeds" to aggregates whose membership shrank** (or same-string members).

    ## U239 — Concrete shutdown defect found by read-only native HA inspection (executor Future cancelled by native async_stop before the stop event, so the final coordinator save is skipped); PYTAP-140-02-FIX assigned to a fresh MAI Code 2 worker; board reconciled

    - Timestamp: 2026-11-25T17:37:49Z (the message's `current_datetime`)
    - Session: 72475d47-6f0e-4206-9387-b6e4948802a4
    - Branch: azebro-legendary-spork
    - Update ID: U239 (follows U238)
    - Task: PYTAP-140-02 (In Progress), PYTAP-140-02-FIX (new), PYTAP-140-05 (Blocked). Everything below is coordinator-reported; I audited nothing.

    ### Finding (VERIFIED by read-only inspection of native HA 2026.11.2, coordinator-reported)
    - **`HomeAssistant.async_add_executor_job` registers the returned executor Future in `_background_tasks` when called from our background `_async_listen`.**
    - **Native `async_stop` cancels all those Futures BEFORE `EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_STOP`.**
    - **Shielding `_worker_future` protects only against waiter cancellation, NOT against this direct native cancellation.**
    - **The existing tracked stop handler then awaits the cancelled `_worker_future`; the resulting `CancelledError` skips the final coordinator save.**
    - **The coordinator states this explains the stale counters** (the U238 restored-lower counters). **A faithful RED regression is still required** (explanation not yet proven by a test).
    - **Reproducible via installed Core `inspect.getsource(HomeAssistant.async_stop / async_add_executor_job)`.**

    ### Repository and board state (coordinator-reported)
    - **`origin` fetched successfully. HEAD remains `3ea5aedc`;** only the journal and board were initially dirty.
    - **The board now reconciles:** the explicit user rollback/fix approval; the completed exact-#39 rollback (17:12:32Z; native confirmation 17:15:08Z); PYTAP-140-02 In Progress; PYTAP-140-05 blocked on the correction and a new soak.
    - **The private failed-soak JSON adds recovery references** while retaining the failed clock and the old failure reason.

    ### Delegation (ATTEMPTED, coordinator-reported)
    - **Fresh MAI Code 2 worker `7f4a52f6-7ec3-4a22-9a20-64d276343b28` assigned PYTAP-140-02-FIX.**
    - **Exclusive ownership:** `coordinator.py`, `test_listener_lifecycle.py`, `core_smoke.py`.
    - **Requirements:** TDD; an actual native stop plus real persistence reproduction; a **minimal public default-executor ownership fix** is proposed; **no aggregate-semantics change; no live operations; no nested delegation.**
    - **The main session owns** docs and operational-artifact preparation, and later reviews and gates.

- Update ID: U264
- Task: PYTAP-140-02 In Progress; PYTAP-140-05 Blocked.

### Code trace and evidence boundary
- Read `custom_components/pytap/sensor.py` around `PyTapAggregateSensor._accumulate_energy` (lines 811–863). For each member with a saved baseline, a lower current value adds the current value to `_energy_total`; otherwise it adds the delta. This preserves post-reset energy.
- That branch is consistent with the approved PRD's documented risk that a stale lower restored member value can be interpreted as a real reset and overcount. It is only a plausible explanation for the Nov 26 live discrepancies, not a confirmed cause.
- The retained pre-deployment snapshots preserve group aggregate sums but not each member's daily/lifetime value before deployment. Available evidence therefore cannot confirm which member values decreased or whether this branch fired.
- The evidence file also records a Nov 25 incident whose recorder history did identify five daily and three cumulative counters restored 0.01 Wh lower. This is separate evidence and does not establish the trigger of the larger Nov 26 discrepancy.
- Updated `docs/evidence/prd-pytap-1.4.0-001-v9-r14-candidate.md` with the code trace, uncertainty, and guard against removing reset credit or applying an arbitrary tolerance.

### State / next action
- No HA state was changed, no new deployment or test was run, and no release/soak was started. Exact #39 remains active; candidate-created discrepancies remain unrepaired.
- Next: obtain a focused multi-member restart reproduction or sanitized per-member before/after evidence, then determine whether a correction fits the approved scope. Keep release blocked until live energy criteria pass.
- No secrets or credentials recorded.
